    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… I guess I’m here because things at work have gotten to where I can’t just brush them off anymore. I do environmental modeling—contract work, a lot of datasets, revisions, tracked changes, that sort of thing—and I’m pretty sure some of the people on this current contract have been watching my revisions more closely than they should be. Not just normal review, I mean… tracking them, using them.

There have been errors showing up in the datasets, and they’re the kind of errors that make me look careless, or like I don’t understand the structure of the model, which is ridiculous because I’ve led this kind of work for years. And then when I point it out, people say, “Oh, it’s probably just version-control issues,” or “somebody overwrote the wrong file,” and my wife says the same thing, and a couple of the junior analysts do too. But it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels planted. Like they’re trying to make it look like I’m not capable of leading the work anymore.

I know how that sounds, but when you see the same kinds of mistakes appear right after your revisions, and always in places tied to your part of the project, it’s hard not to see a pattern. So… that’s what brings me in. I need someone to listen to it without immediately telling me it’s just a software or file-naming problem.

Outside of work… I mean, not in the same concrete way, no. It’s really this contract where it’s obvious to me. That’s where the pattern is. The revisions, the datasets, the timing of the errors showing up—it all lines up too neatly.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of work, no, not really in the sense of someone trying to physically harm me or anything like that. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are after me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… this contract specifically. That’s where I keep coming back to, because that’s where the facts are.

I’m pretty sure the people connected to that environmental modeling project have been tracking my revisions in a way that goes beyond normal review. And then these errors appear in the datasets right afterward—errors that make me look incompetent, like I don’t understand the model structure or can’t lead the work. My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, like someone overwrote a file or named something wrong. But the pattern is too consistent for me to just accept that.

So, outside of work, I wouldn’t say I have those thoughts. But with this project, yes, I do feel like I’m being undermined. Not “briefly,” either—it’s been strong enough that I’m here talking to you about it.

And then when I point it out, people say, “Oh, it’s probably just version-control issues,” or “somebody overwrote the wrong file,” and my wife says the same thing, and a couple of the junior analysts do too. But it doesn’t feel random to me. It feels planted. Like they’re trying to make it look like I’m not capable of leading the work anymore.\n\nI know how that sounds, but when you see the same kinds of mistakes appear right after your revisions, and always in places tied to your part of the project, it’s hard not to see a pattern. So… that’s what brings me in. I need someone to listen to it without immediately telling me it’s just a software or file-naming problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that people were targeting you, watching you, or trying to undermine you outside of work or in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I mean, not in the same concrete way, no. It’s really this contract where it’s obvious to me. That’s where the pattern is. The revisions, the datasets, the timing of the errors showing up—it all lines up too neatly.\n\nI know my wife thinks I’m over-reading it, and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes or someone overwriting files, but that explanation doesn’t hold up when the errors keep landing in the areas tied to my part of the model. It’s not just one mistake. It’s the type of mistake, where it appears, and when it appears—right after my revisions, usually in a way that makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nSo if you mean people on the street or neighbors or something, I’m not saying that. But with the people connected to this work, yes, I do feel targeted. I feel like my work is being watched more closely than normal review, and like things are being planted in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the project anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that others were trying to harm you or undermine you in situations outside of work, even if only briefly or during times of stress?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, no, not really in the sense of someone trying to physically harm me or anything like that. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are after me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… this contract specifically. That’s where I keep coming back to, because that’s where the facts are.\n\nI’m pretty sure the people connected to that environmental modeling project have been tracking my revisions in a way that goes beyond normal review. And then these errors appear in the datasets right afterward—errors that make me look incompetent, like I don’t understand the model structure or can’t lead the work. My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, like someone overwrote a file or named something wrong. But the pattern is too consistent for me to just accept that.\n\nSo, outside of work, I wouldn’t say I have those thoughts. But with this project, yes, I do feel like I’m being undermined. Not “briefly,” either—it’s been strong enough that I’m here talking to you about it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else or that thoughts were being placed in your mind that weren’t your own?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "patient_turn_index: 4\n(If 1, this is your first reply — return your chief complaint.)\nReturn the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think so. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.

There are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.

Sometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.

I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.\n\nThere are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / mathematical_science_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the work, even when my wife or the junior analysts tell me it’s just version-control mistakes.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.

But with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.

My wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.

I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / mathematical_science_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the work, even when my wife or the junior analysts tell me it’s just version-control mistakes.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. It’s not like I walk down the street and think a sign or a song or something on the news is meant for me personally. I don’t really think that way.

It’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling work. That’s where it feels… directed, I guess. Because it keeps happening around my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless little mix-ups to me. They’re the kind of errors that make me look incompetent, like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand the structure. And I know my wife says it sounds like version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things too, like files getting overwritten or people working from the wrong copy. I get that explanation in theory.

But when it keeps lining up with what I changed, it feels very hard to believe it’s just accidental. That’s why I keep coming back to the idea that people on the contract are tracking my revisions and planting those mistakes. Outside of that, though, no—not really. It’s the work situation that feels targeted at me.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really with friends or family. I mean, my wife will say things that feel like she’s trying to get me to calm down about the contract, but I don’t think she’s sending secret messages or anything like that. She’s pretty direct, actually. She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.

The part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure.

So in my personal life, no, I don’t think friends or family are secretly aiming things at me. It’s more that they don’t understand why I’m so concerned about what’s happening with the files. But the contract situation itself… that feels targeted.

No, outside of the contract, I don’t really have that feeling. I don’t think random things are connected to me in that way. Like if someone says something at the grocery store or I see something on television, I don’t take it as a message for me or anything like that.

My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, like someone overwrote a file or named something wrong. But the pattern is too consistent for me to just accept that.\n\nSo, outside of work, I wouldn’t say I have those thoughts. But with this project, yes, I do feel like I’m being undermined. Not “briefly,” either—it’s been strong enough that I’m here talking to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else or that thoughts were being placed in your mind that weren’t your own?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in that sense. I mean, I get stuck on things, obviously, and I go over the work situation in my head a lot, probably more than my wife would like. But it still feels like my own thinking. It’s me looking at what happened and trying to make sense of it.\n\nI’ve never had the feeling that somebody was, you know, putting a thought into my head like it wasn’t mine, or steering my mind from the outside. That sounds different from what I’m talking about. I can question whether people are manipulating the files or watching revisions, but my thoughts about it feel like my thoughts. They’re not being controlled. At least, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being broadcast out loud, or that others could somehow hear your thoughts or know what you were thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.\n\nThere are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.\n\nBut with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. It’s not like I walk down the street and think a sign or a song or something on the news is meant for me personally. I don’t really think that way.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling work. That’s where it feels… directed, I guess. Because it keeps happening around my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless little mix-ups to me. They’re the kind of errors that make me look incompetent, like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand the structure. And I know my wife says it sounds like version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things too, like files getting overwritten or people working from the wrong copy. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think it’s because I have some special power or secret knowledge or anything like that. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that people are watching me outside of my work.

It’s more specific than that. It’s because of my role on this environmental modeling contract and the revisions I’m making. I’m here because I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking those revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward. And those errors aren’t random, at least not the way they look to me. They show up in places that make me look like I can’t lead the work or that I don’t understand the model structure.

My wife keeps telling me it could just be version-control problems, and the junior analysts say things like files were overwritten or someone used the wrong copy. I understand that explanation, I really do. I’ve worked with messy files long enough to know that happens. But when the mistakes keep appearing right after I change something, and they damage how my work looks, it feels targeted. So no, not because I’m uniquely important beyond the job. It’s tied to the contract and what I’m responsible for there.

They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. And those errors aren’t random, at least not the way they look to me. They show up in places that make me look like I can’t lead the work or that I don’t understand the model structure.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it could just be version-control problems, and the junior analysts say things like files were overwritten or someone used the wrong copy. I understand that explanation, I really do. I’ve worked with messy files long enough to know that happens. But when the mistakes keep appearing right after I change something, and they damage how my work looks, it feels targeted. So no, not because I’m uniquely important beyond the job. It’s tied to the contract and what I’m responsible for there.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had beliefs or ideas that others found strange or unfounded, even when there was no evidence to support them, outside of this work situation?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / mathematical_science_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions and planting errors in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the work, even when my wife or the junior analysts tell me it’s just version-control mistakes.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Outside of the work situation? No, I don’t think so. I mean, people might say this contract thing sounds unfounded, and I understand why they say that, because they keep bringing up version control and overwritten files and all that. My wife especially keeps saying, “This could just be a file-management problem,” and the junior analysts have said similar things.

But that’s the part I keep coming back to—it isn’t some random idea I have about everything. It’s specifically this environmental modeling contract. I make revisions, and then afterward errors appear in the datasets in ways that make me look incompetent, like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure. And I know people think I’m reading too much into it, but it feels very hard to dismiss when it keeps lining up that way.

So, no, I can’t really think of strange beliefs outside of that. It’s this one situation that people don’t seem to believe me about.

No, not supernatural, no. I don’t think I have abilities like that, and I’m not saying some outside force is talking to me or sending me messages. That’s not what this is.

What I’ve been trying to explain is more concrete than that, at least to me. It’s about the environmental modeling contract. I make revisions to the model or the datasets, and then after that, errors show up—specific errors, in places that reflect badly on my judgment or make it look like I don’t understand how to lead the work. And people keep telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, or someone overwrote a file, or used an outdated copy. My wife says that, and the junior analysts say that too. I do understand that those things happen.

But when it keeps happening after my revisions, and the result is that I look incompetent, it’s hard for me to accept that it’s just ordinary mistakes. So, no, I wouldn’t say I believe in anything impossible or outside reality. I think people on that contract are tracking my changes and planting errors to make me look bad. That may sound extreme to other people, but it doesn’t feel supernatural to me—it feels like something people could actually do.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not in other parts of my life. I know I keep circling back to the same thing, but that’s because that’s the only place where this has really happened for me.

I’m not walking around thinking impossible things are happening in general. I don’t think there’s some force or anything like that, and I don’t have those kinds of beliefs outside work. It’s this environmental modeling contract. That’s where I’m pretty sure people have been tracking my revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward, in ways that make it look like I can’t lead the work or that I don’t understand what I’m doing.

And I know how it sounds when I say it. My wife keeps telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say files get overwritten or the wrong copy gets used. I understand that explanation in theory. I really do. But when the errors keep showing up right after I make changes, and they always seem to damage my credibility, it’s hard for me to accept that it’s just random. So outside of that, no, I can’t think of anything unrealistic like that. It’s this one work situation.

I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.\n\nBut with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. It’s the work situation that feels targeted at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people said or did in your personal life, like with friends or family, were secretly directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really with friends or family. I mean, my wife will say things that feel like she’s trying to get me to calm down about the contract, but I don’t think she’s sending secret messages or anything like that. She’s pretty direct, actually. She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.\n\nThe part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure.\n\nSo in my personal life, no, I don’t think friends or family are secretly aiming things at me. It’s more that they don’t understand why I’m so concerned about what’s happening with the files. But the contract situation itself… that feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your work on this contract, have you ever felt that random or unrelated events—like things people said, things you saw, or things that happened—were specifically connected to you or had a hidden meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: No, outside of the contract, I don’t really have that feeling. I don’t think random things are connected to me in that way. Like if someone says something at the grocery store or I see something on television, I don’t take it as a message for me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s this work situation that keeps bothering me. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and the way those errors appear makes it look like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand what I’m doing. My wife says it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say similar things, like files getting overwritten or people using the wrong copy. It’s really centered on that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have special abilities, knowledge, or importance that others don’t, or that you are being watched because of something unique about you beyond your professional role?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s because I have some special power or secret knowledge or anything like that. It’s specifically this environmental modeling contract. I make revisions, and then afterward errors appear in the datasets in ways that make me look incompetent, like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure. It’s this one situation that people don’t seem to believe me about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed in something that others would consider impossible or completely outside the bounds of reality, like having supernatural abilities or being directly communicated by some external force?\n\nPatient: No, not supernatural, no. I don’t think I have abilities like that, and I’m not saying some outside force is talking to me or sending me messages. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I’ve been trying to explain is more concrete than that, at least to me. It’s about the environmental modeling contract. I don’t think it’s without a reason or context, no. That’s the distinction I’m trying to make, because I know it probably sounds like I’m just suspicious out of nowhere, but to me it’s tied to a very specific situation.

I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward, so it looks like I can’t lead the work properly. My wife keeps telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts have said the same sort of thing, like files getting overwritten or someone using the wrong copy. I understand that those things can happen. I’ve worked around data and modeling long enough to know mistakes happen. But this feels too targeted to me. The timing, the way the errors reflect on my decisions—it just doesn’t feel accidental.

So, no, I wouldn’t say it’s without any reason or context. The context is the contract. That’s where it’s happening.

She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.\n\nThe part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure.\n\nSo in my personal life, no, I don’t think friends or family are secretly aiming things at me. It’s more that they don’t understand why I’m so concerned about what’s happening with the files. But the contract situation itself… that feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your work on this contract, have you ever felt that random or unrelated events—like things people said, things you saw, or things that happened—were specifically connected to you or had a hidden meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: No, outside of the contract, I don’t really have that feeling. I don’t think random things are connected to me in that way. Like if someone says something at the grocery store or I see something on television, I don’t take it as a message for me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s this work situation that keeps bothering me. I know I’ve probably said it already, but I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and the way those errors appear makes it look like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand what I’m doing. My wife says it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say similar things, like files getting overwritten or people using the wrong copy. I understand what they’re saying, technically. I do.\n\nBut when it keeps happening after my revisions, it feels too specific to ignore. So, no, not random events outside work. It’s really centered on that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have special abilities, knowledge, or importance that others don’t, or that you are being watched because of something unique about you beyond your professional role?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s because I have some special power or secret knowledge or anything like that. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that people are watching me outside of my work.\n\nIt’s more specific than that. It’s because of my role on this environmental modeling contract and the revisions I’m making. I’m here because I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking those revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward. And those errors aren’t random, at least not the way they look to me. They show up in places that make me look like I can’t lead the work or that I don’t understand the model structure.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it could just be version-control problems, and the junior analysts say things like files were overwritten or someone used the wrong copy. I understand that explanation, I really do. I’ve worked with messy files long enough to know that happens. But when the mistakes keep appearing right after I change something, and they damage how my work looks, it feels targeted. So no, not because I’m uniquely important beyond the job. It’s tied to the contract and what I’m responsible for there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others found strange or unfounded, even when there was no evidence to support them, outside of this work situation?\n\nPatient: Outside of the work situation? No, I don’t think so. I mean, people might say this contract thing sounds unfounded, and I understand why they say that, because they keep bringing up version control and overwritten files and all that. My wife especially keeps saying, “This could just be a file-management problem,” and the junior analysts have said similar things.\n\nBut that’s the part I keep coming back to—it isn’t some random idea I have about everything. It’s specifically this environmental modeling contract. I make revisions, and then afterward errors appear in the datasets in ways that make me look incompetent, like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure. And I know people think I’m reading too much into it, but it feels very hard to dismiss when it keeps lining up that way.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really think of strange beliefs outside of that. It’s this one situation that people don’t seem to believe me about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed in something that others would consider impossible or completely outside the bounds of reality, like having supernatural abilities or being directly communicated by some external force?\n\nPatient: No, not supernatural, no. I don’t think I have abilities like that, and I’m not saying some outside force is talking to me or sending me messages. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I’ve been trying to explain is more concrete than that, at least to me. It’s about the environmental modeling contract. I make revisions to the model or the datasets, and then after that, errors show up—specific errors, in places that reflect badly on my judgment or make it look like I don’t understand how to lead the work. And people keep telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, or someone overwrote a file, or used an outdated copy. My wife says that, and the junior analysts say that too. I do understand that those things happen.\n\nBut when it keeps happening after my revisions, and the result is that I look incompetent, it’s hard for me to accept that it’s just ordinary mistakes. So, no, I wouldn’t say I believe in anything impossible or outside reality. I think people on that contract are tracking my changes and planting errors to make me look bad. That may sound extreme to other people, but it doesn’t feel supernatural to me—it feels like something people could actually do.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or concerns that others would consider completely unrealistic or impossible, even in other parts of your life, not related to work?\n\nPatient: No, not in other parts of my life. I don’t think there’s some force or anything like that, and I don’t have those kinds of beliefs outside work. It’s this environmental modeling contract. That’s where I’m pretty sure people have been tracking my revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward, in ways that make it look like I can’t lead the work or that I don’t understand what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know how it sounds when I say it. My wife keeps telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say files get overwritten or the wrong copy gets used. I understand that explanation in theory. I really do. But when the errors keep showing up right after I make changes, and they always seem to damage my credibility, it’s hard for me to accept that it’s just random. So outside of that, no, I can’t think of anything unrealistic like that. It’s this one work situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or suspicions that something was happening or being done to you without any clear reason or context at all, even when there was no evidence or situation that would logically support it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s without a reason or context, no. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, that’s closer to what I mean. Not something impossible, but something that could actually happen. People can be dishonest at work. People can cover themselves, shift blame, make someone else look bad. That’s not fantasy, you know?

With this environmental modeling contract, I’m pretty sure people have been watching or tracking the revisions I make, and then after that, errors get planted in the datasets. And the errors aren’t just random little typos. They show up in places that make it look like I don’t understand the model or that I can’t lead the work. It affects how people see my judgment.

My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control problems, or someone using the wrong file, and the junior analysts say the same kind of thing—files overwritten, outdated copies, mistakes like that. I understand those explanations. I really do. I’ve worked with datasets long enough to know mistakes happen. But the timing is what bothers me. I make revisions, then errors appear, and they’re the kind of errors that reflect badly on me specifically.

So, yes, to me it seems reasonable. Other people don’t seem to see it that way, or they act like I’m overreading it. But from where I’m sitting, it looks like someone is taking advantage of the confusion in the files to make me look incompetent.

It’s not like I woke up one day and thought, “Everyone’s against me,” with no connection to anything. It’s the pattern around that contract—my changes go in, and then these errors show up in places that make me look careless or incompetent.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts have said the same sort of thing, like files getting overwritten or someone using the wrong copy. I understand that those things can happen. I’ve worked around data and modeling long enough to know mistakes happen. But this feels too targeted to me. The timing, the way the errors reflect on my decisions—it just doesn’t feel accidental.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say it’s without any reason or context. The context is the contract. That’s where it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had suspicions or beliefs about something happening to you that seemed completely reasonable to you, even if others didn’t see it the same way—like someone being dishonest, cheating, or trying to take advantage of you in a way that could actually happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes, that’s closer to what I mean.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I mean, yes, it has pretty much stayed the same, even when they give those explanations.

I do hear what they’re saying. My wife will say, “This sounds like version-control issues,” or that someone probably overwrote a file by accident, and the junior analysts say things like that too—wrong copy, outdated dataset, someone saving over something. And I’m not saying those things are impossible. I know they happen. I’ve worked with models and datasets long enough to know files can get messy.

But that doesn’t really explain it for me. The part I can’t get past is the timing and the pattern. I make revisions, then after that errors show up in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless mistakes. They’re the kind of errors that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, or that I can’t lead the contract properly. It feels like someone is tracking what I change and then planting problems afterward so the blame lands on me.

So, yes, even after people tell me it’s probably accidental or technical, I still feel pretty sure that’s what’s happening. I don’t just drop it because their explanation doesn’t account for how targeted it feels.

And then these errors appear in the datasets right afterward—errors that make me look incompetent, like I don’t understand the model structure or can’t lead the work. My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, like someone overwrote a file or named something wrong. But the pattern is too consistent for me to just accept that.\n\nSo, outside of work, I wouldn’t say I have those thoughts. But with this project, yes, I do feel like I’m being undermined. Not “briefly,” either—it’s been strong enough that I’m here talking to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else or that thoughts were being placed in your mind that weren’t your own?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in that sense. I mean, I get stuck on things, obviously, and I go over the work situation in my head a lot, probably more than my wife would like. But it still feels like my own thinking. It’s me looking at what happened and trying to make sense of it.\n\nI’ve never had the feeling that somebody was, you know, putting a thought into my head like it wasn’t mine, or steering my mind from the outside. That sounds different from what I’m talking about. I can question whether people are manipulating the files or watching revisions, but my thoughts about it feel like my thoughts. They’re not being controlled. At least, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being broadcast out loud, or that others could somehow hear your thoughts or know what you were thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.\n\nThere are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.\n\nBut with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. It’s not like I walk down the street and think a sign or a song or something on the news is meant for me personally. I don’t really think that way.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling work. That’s where it feels… directed, I guess. Because it keeps happening around my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless little mix-ups to me. They’re the kind of errors that make me look incompetent, like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand the structure. And I know my wife says it sounds like version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things too, like files getting overwritten or people working from the wrong copy. I get that explanation in theory.\n\nBut when it keeps lining up with what I changed, it feels very hard to believe it’s just accidental. That’s why I keep coming back to the idea that people on the contract are tracking my revisions and planting those mistakes. Outside of that, though, no—not really. It’s the work situation that feels targeted at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people said or did in your personal life, like with friends or family, were secretly directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really with friends or family. I mean, my wife will say things that feel like she’s trying to get me to calm down about the contract, but I don’t think she’s sending secret messages or anything like that. She’s pretty direct, actually. She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.\n\nThe part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Absolutely certain… I don’t know if I’d use that exact phrase, because then it sounds like I’m saying I’ve got a signed confession or some camera footage, and I don’t. I’m not claiming I have that kind of proof.

But am I pretty sure? Yes. I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward, specifically so it looks like I can’t lead the work. That’s the part I keep coming back to.

My wife says version-control. The junior analysts say files get overwritten, wrong copy, old dataset, all of that. And I understand those are real things. I’m not ignorant about how messy datasets can get. But those explanations don’t cover the pattern. If it were just ordinary file confusion, I’d expect the errors to be more random, or to affect everyone equally, or to show up in ways that don’t always land on my decisions.

Instead, it keeps lining up with my revisions and making me look careless or incompetent. So no, I don’t really believe version-control mistakes are the real cause, at least not for the main things I’m seeing. It feels intentional. It feels targeted.

It’s me looking at what happened and trying to make sense of it.\n\nI’ve never had the feeling that somebody was, you know, putting a thought into my head like it wasn’t mine, or steering my mind from the outside. That sounds different from what I’m talking about. I can question whether people are manipulating the files or watching revisions, but my thoughts about it feel like my thoughts. They’re not being controlled. At least, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being broadcast out loud, or that others could somehow hear your thoughts or know what you were thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.\n\nThere are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.\n\nBut with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. It’s not like I walk down the street and think a sign or a song or something on the news is meant for me personally. I don’t really think that way.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling work. That’s where it feels… directed, I guess. Because it keeps happening around my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless little mix-ups to me. They’re the kind of errors that make me look incompetent, like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand the structure. And I know my wife says it sounds like version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things too, like files getting overwritten or people working from the wrong copy. I get that explanation in theory.\n\nBut when it keeps lining up with what I changed, it feels very hard to believe it’s just accidental. That’s why I keep coming back to the idea that people on the contract are tracking my revisions and planting those mistakes. Outside of that, though, no—not really. It’s the work situation that feels targeted at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people said or did in your personal life, like with friends or family, were secretly directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really with friends or family. I mean, my wife will say things that feel like she’s trying to get me to calm down about the contract, but I don’t think she’s sending secret messages or anything like that. She’s pretty direct, actually. She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.\n\nThe part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure.\n\nSo in my personal life, no, I don’t think friends or family are secretly aiming things at me. It’s more that they don’t understand why I’m so concerned about what’s happening with the files. But the contract situation itself… that feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your work on this contract, have you ever felt that random or unrelated events—like things people said, things you saw, or things that happened—were specifically connected to you or had a hidden meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: No, outside of the contract, I don’t really have that feeling. I don’t think random things are connected to me in that way. Like if someone says something at the grocery store or I see something on television, I don’t take it as a message for me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s this work situation that keeps bothering me. I know I’ve probably said it already, but I’m here because I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and the way those errors appear makes it look like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand what I’m doing. My wife says it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say similar things, like files getting overwritten or people using the wrong copy. I understand what they’re saying, technically. I do.\n\nBut when it keeps happening after my revisions, it feels too specific to ignore. So, no, not random events outside work. It’s really centered on that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have special abilities, knowledge, or importance that others don’t, or that you are being watched because of something unique about you beyond your professional role?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s because I have some special power or secret knowledge or anything like that. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that people are watching me outside of my work.\n\nIt’s more specific than that. It’s because of my role on this environmental modeling contract and the revisions I’m making. I understand that explanation, I really do. I’ve worked with messy files long enough to know that happens. But when the mistakes keep appearing right after I change something, and they damage how my work looks, it feels targeted. So no, not because I’m uniquely important beyond the job. It’s tied to the contract and what I’m responsible for there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others found strange or unfounded, even when there was no evidence to support them, outside of this work situation?\n\nPatient: Outside of the work situation? No, I don’t think so. I mean, people might say this contract thing sounds unfounded, and I understand why they say that, because they keep bringing up version control and overwritten files and all that. My wife especially keeps saying, “This could just be a file-management problem,” and the junior analysts have said similar things.\n\nBut that’s the part I keep coming back to—it isn’t some random idea I have about everything. It’s specifically this environmental modeling contract. I make revisions, and then afterward errors appear in the datasets in ways that make me look incompetent, like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure. And I know people think I’m reading too much into it, but it feels very hard to dismiss when it keeps lining up that way.\n\nSo, no, I can’t really think of strange beliefs outside of that. It’s this one situation that people don’t seem to believe me about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed in something that others would consider impossible or completely outside the bounds of reality, like having supernatural abilities or being directly communicated by some external force?\n\nPatient: No, not supernatural, no. I don’t think I have abilities like that, and I’m not saying some outside force is talking to me or sending me messages. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I’ve been trying to explain is more concrete than that, at least to me. It’s about the environmental modeling contract. It’s not like I woke up one day and thought, “Everyone’s against me,” with no connection to anything. The revisions, the datasets, the timing of the errors showing up—it all lines up too neatly.\n\nI know my wife thinks I’m over-reading it, and the junior analysts keep saying it’s just version-control mistakes or someone overwriting files, but that explanation doesn’t hold up when the errors keep landing in the areas tied to my part of the model. It’s not just one mistake. It’s the type of mistake, where it appears, and when it appears—right after my revisions, usually in a way that makes me look like I don’t know what I’m doing.\n\nSo if you mean people on the street or neighbors or something, I’m not saying that. But with the people connected to this work, yes, I do feel targeted. I feel like my work is being watched more closely than normal review, and like things are being planted in the datasets to make it look like I can’t lead the project anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that others were trying to harm you or undermine you in situations outside of work, even if only briefly or during times of stress?\n\nPatient: Outside of work, no, not really in the sense of someone trying to physically harm me or anything like that. I don’t walk around thinking strangers are after me, if that’s what you mean. It’s more… this contract specifically. That’s where I keep coming back to, because that’s where the facts are.\n\nI’m pretty sure the people connected to that environmental modeling project have been tracking my revisions in a way that goes beyond normal review. And then these errors appear in the datasets right afterward—errors that make me look incompetent, like I don’t understand the model structure or can’t lead the work. My wife keeps saying it’s probably version-control mistakes, and the junior analysts say the same thing, like someone overwrote a file or named something wrong. But the pattern is too consistent for me to just accept that.\n\nSo, outside of work, I wouldn’t say I have those thoughts. But with this project, yes, I do feel like I’m being undermined. Not “briefly,” either—it’s been strong enough that I’m here talking to you about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else or that thoughts were being placed in your mind that weren’t your own?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not in that sense. I mean, I get stuck on things, obviously, and I go over the work situation in my head a lot, probably more than my wife would like. But it still feels like my own thinking. It’s me looking at what happened and trying to make sense of it.\n\nI’ve never had the feeling that somebody was, you know, putting a thought into my head like it wasn’t mine, or steering my mind from the outside. That sounds different from what I’m talking about. I can question whether people are manipulating the files or watching revisions, but my thoughts about it feel like my thoughts. They’re not being controlled. At least, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being broadcast out loud, or that others could somehow hear your thoughts or know what you were thinking without you saying it?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I understand what you’re asking, like whether I’ve felt my thoughts were somehow going out where other people could hear them, or that someone could read my mind without me speaking. I haven’t had that feeling.\n\nThere are times when I think people are interpreting my work or watching what I’m doing too closely, but that’s not the same as hearing my thoughts. If someone knows what I’m thinking, it’s usually because I’ve said something in a meeting, written it in comments, or it’s obvious from the changes I made. I don’t feel like my private thoughts are being broadcast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I do wonder if people can anticipate what I’m going to change in the model, but that’s based on the files and the timing, not because they’re inside my head. So… no, not in the sense you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like conversations, media, or events—were specifically directed at you or had a special meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like the TV is talking to me or strangers’ conversations are secretly about me, no. I wouldn’t put it that way.\n\nBut with the contract, yes, I do feel like certain things that happen around that work are directed at me specifically. That’s actually why I’m here. I’m pretty sure the people on that environmental modeling contract have been tracking my revisions—watching what I change in the model—and then somehow errors show up in the datasets afterward. And they’re not random-looking errors, at least not to me. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know what I’m doing, like I can’t manage the structure of the model or lead the project properly.\n\nMy wife keeps telling me it’s probably just version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things, like maybe files were overwritten or mislabeled. I hear that, I do. But when it keeps lining up with my revisions, it starts to feel very pointed. So I don’t think every conversation or every event has a special meaning for me, but the things happening around this project… those feel aimed at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that only the events related to your work on this contract have a special meaning or are directed at you, or do you also feel that other events outside of work—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it spreads into everything else. It’s not like I walk down the street and think a sign or a song or something on the news is meant for me personally. I don’t really think that way.\n\nIt’s mainly this contract. The environmental modeling work. That’s where it feels… directed, I guess. Because it keeps happening around my revisions. I’ll make changes to the model, and then later there are errors in the datasets, and they’re not just harmless little mix-ups to me. They’re the kind of errors that make me look incompetent, like I can’t lead the work or don’t understand the structure. And I know my wife says it sounds like version-control problems, and the junior analysts have said similar things too, like files getting overwritten or people working from the wrong copy. I get that explanation in theory.\n\nBut when it keeps lining up with what I changed, it feels very hard to believe it’s just accidental. That’s why I keep coming back to the idea that people on the contract are tracking my revisions and planting those mistakes. Outside of that, though, no—not really. It’s the work situation that feels targeted at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people said or did in your personal life, like with friends or family, were secretly directed at you or had a hidden meaning meant just for you?\n\nPatient: No, not really with friends or family. I mean, my wife will say things that feel like she’s trying to get me to calm down about the contract, but I don’t think she’s sending secret messages or anything like that. She’s pretty direct, actually. She’ll say, “Maybe it’s just version control,” or that the junior analysts probably overwrote something or used the wrong file, and I know what she means.\n\nThe part that feels hidden or directed is the work itself—the environmental modeling contract. That’s why I’m here. I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking my revisions, watching what I change in the model, and then these errors show up in the datasets afterward. And the errors aren’t neutral, at least they don’t feel neutral. They’re the kind that make it look like I don’t know how to lead the project or manage the model structure.\n\nSo in my personal life, no, I don’t think friends or family are secretly aiming things at me. It’s more that they don’t understand why I’m so concerned about what’s happening with the files. But the contract situation itself… that feels targeted.\n\nDoctor: Outside of your work on this contract, have you ever felt that random or unrelated events—like things people said, things you saw, or things that happened—were specifically connected to you or had a hidden meaning for you personally?\n\nPatient: No, outside of the contract, I don’t really have that feeling. I don’t think random things are connected to me in that way. Like if someone says something at the grocery store or I see something on television, I don’t take it as a message for me or anything like that.\n\nIt’s this work situation that keeps bothering me. I understand what they’re saying, technically. I do.\n\nBut when it keeps happening after my revisions, it feels too specific to ignore. So, no, not random events outside work. It’s really centered on that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that you have special abilities, knowledge, or importance that others don’t, or that you are being watched because of something unique about you beyond your professional role?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s because I have some special power or secret knowledge or anything like that. I’m not saying I’m important in some grand way, or that people are watching me outside of my work.\n\nIt’s more specific than that. It’s because of my role on this environmental modeling contract and the revisions I’m making. I’m here because I’m pretty sure people on that contract have been tracking those revisions and then planting errors in the datasets afterward. It’s tied to the contract and what I’m responsible for there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that others found strange or unfounded, even when there was no evidence to support them, outside of this work situation?\n\nPatient: Outside of the work situation? No, I don’t think so. I mean, people might say this contract thing sounds unfounded, and I understand why they say that, because they keep bringing up version control and overwritten files and all that. My wife especially keeps saying, “This could just be a file-management problem,” and the junior analysts have said similar things.\n\nBut that’s the part I keep coming back to—it isn’t some random idea I have about everything. It’s this one situation that people don’t seem to believe me about.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed in something that others would consider impossible or completely outside the bounds of reality, like having supernatural abilities or being directly communicated by some external force?\n\nPatient: No, not supernatural, no. I don’t think I have abilities like that, and I’m not saying some outside force is talking to me or sending me messages. That’s not what this is.\n\nWhat I’ve been trying to explain is more concrete than that, at least to me. It’s about the environmental modeling contract. It feels like someone is tracking what I change and then planting problems afterward so the blame lands on me.\n\nSo, yes, even after people tell me it’s probably accidental or technical, I still feel pretty sure that’s what’s happening. I don’t just drop it because their explanation doesn’t account for how targeted it feels.\n\nDoctor: Even though others have given you alternate explanations for the errors in the dataset, like version-control issues or files being overwritten, do you feel absolutely certain that these are not the real cause and that someone is intentionally targeting your work?\n\nPatient: Absolutely certain… I don’t know if I’d use that exact phrase, because then it sounds like I’m saying I’ve got a signed confession or some camera footage, and I don’t. I’m not claiming I have that kind of proof.\n\nBut am I pretty sure? Yes. That’s the part I keep coming back to.\n\nMy wife says version-control. The junior analysts say files get overwritten, wrong copy, old dataset, all of that. And I understand those are real things. I’m not ignorant about how messy datasets can get. But those explanations don’t cover the pattern. If it were just ordinary file confusion, I’d expect the errors to be more random, or to affect everyone equally, or to show up in ways that don’t always land on my decisions.\n\nInstead, it keeps lining up with my revisions and making me look careless or incompetent. I mean, my wife disagrees with me about it, but I don’t think she’s part of it. She keeps telling me it’s probably version-control mistakes or files being overwritten, and I know she’s trying to be practical.\n\nFor me it’s really centered on the contract and the people who have access to the environmental modeling work. That’s what feels targeted.\n\nSo outside of that… I wouldn’t say I have some broad feeling that everyone is against me. It’s more that the people connected to the contract, or the files, or the workflow, are tracking what I change and then using the confusion around versions to make me look incompetent. My wife and the junior analysts can call it accidental, but I don’t think that explains what’s happening."
